# Progress — Era V: Unification (Waves 24-90+, ~Apr 2026 - May 2026)

Archived from PROGRESS.md on 2026-05-08. Spans the run from the AD-507 / AD-509
crew-development bootstrap through the unified knowledge-graph stack (AD-686 /
687 / 688 / 689 / 690 / 691 / 692 / 695), the holodeck/team-simulation layer
(AD-486 / 510 / 512 / 539b / 597), the records-knowledge-browser surface
(AD-562 / AD-520), the AD-641 brain-enhancement umbrella (641a-g + 641g-1),
the commercial-overlay extension seam (AD-697 / 698), and the May 7 roadmap
audit cleanup that closed 37 issues across waves 112-128.

Authoritative wave roster lives in `prompts/wave-plan.yaml`; this file is
historical record only.

----

Build hygiene — Issue #770 CLOSED. UI bundle code-split. Converts `ui/src/main.tsx` `App` import to `React.lazy` boundary + adds `build.rollupOptions.output.manualChunks` to `ui/vite.config.ts` grouping `three` / `@pixiv/three-vrm` into `avatar-vendor` and canvas/profile-avatar app code into `avatar-app`. Both required: `manualChunks` alone is insufficient because `main.tsx` statically imported `App`, so Rollup absorbed three.js back into the entry graph unconditionally (Compact mode paid the full ~3.3 MB cost). Before: single `index-*.js` = 3290.80 KB. After: `index-*.js` = 52.58 KB (gzip 16.75) + `App-*.js` = 439.65 KB (gzip 116.32) + `avatar-app-*.js` = 876.25 KB (gzip 274.75) + `avatar-vendor-*.js` = 1993.00 KB (gzip 517.49). Main entry 62.6× smaller; three.js stack now lazy and skipped entirely on Compact mode. Vitest 934 passed (1 skipped) unchanged. No new tests required — build hygiene, no behavioral change. Suspense fallback `null` (existing WelcomeOverlay handles first paint). Files modified: `ui/src/main.tsx`, `ui/vite.config.ts`. Closes GH issue #770.

AD-723 v1 CLOSED (Wave 144). Sensorium dispatch unification — producer side. Closes GH issue #581. Converts `CognitiveAgent.SENSORIUM_REGISTRY` from `ClassVar[dict[str, tuple[SensoriumLayer, str]]]` inventory to `ClassVar[dict[str, SensoriumEntry]]` dispatch table. New `SensoriumPath` `StrEnum` declares 5 prompt-assembly paths (CHAIN_BASELINE / CHAIN_EXTENSIONS / CHAIN_SITUATION / DM_ONESHOT / WR_ONESHOT). New `SensoriumEntry` frozen dataclass carries `layer` / `description` / `paths` tuple / `priority` int / `output_key` str-or-None. Two dispatcher methods on `CognitiveAgent` (`_dispatch_sensorium_sync` for chain paths — refuses async-registered methods as defense in depth — and `_dispatch_sensorium_async` for DM/WR paths, awaits coroutines, calls sync methods directly). Three chain shims (`_build_cognitive_baseline` / `_build_cognitive_extensions` / `_build_situation_awareness`) refactored to thin dispatcher calls; `_build_cognitive_state` uses single-shared-dict variant so AD-646 None-removal in extensions correctly pops baseline-set keys (e.g. `_no_episodic_memories` when proactive context_parts carries memories). ~22 inline blocks across the three chain methods extracted into named `_sensorium_*` helper methods + 4 wrapper methods for legacy helpers whose signatures predate the dispatcher contract (`_sensorium_temporal_context` / `_sensorium_working_memory` / `_sensorium_comm_proficiency` / `_sensorium_self_recognition`). AD-722 `_build_avatar_self_observation` and AD-722a `_build_intent_self_tag_instruction` register at `paths=(CHAIN_BASELINE, DM_ONESHOT)` and explicitly NOT `WR_ONESHOT` per AD-722 addendum (h) — the motivating dual-wire cases. Future sensorium ADs edit the registry once (one `paths` tuple) and chain-side dispatch picks them up automatically. **Scope reframe per Wave-10 entanglement rule**: the ~415 inline lines across the DM/WR branches of `_build_user_message` carry too much byte-equality risk to land in one commit alongside the registry rewrite — DM/WR consumer-side migration deferred to AD-723a-1 (#617) as a forced-followup. Producer-side already delivers the dual-wire-tax elimination. **Acceptance gate**: golden-text snapshot fixture at `tests/fixtures/sensorium_snapshots/chain_baseline.txt` captured pre-refactor under default config (avatar `inject_into_agent_context=False`, `divergence_detection=False`); post-refactor dispatcher output is byte-identical (`test_chain_baseline_byte_equality`). **Dispatcher convention**: methods return `str` (sets `output_key`), `dict[str, str]` (multi-key contribution), `None` (AD-646 explicit removal — pops the key), or `""` (no contribution — skipped without pop). Internal audit caught that baseline override methods (`_sensorium_source_attribution_rich`, `_sensorium_ext_*`) initially returned `None` for "no opinion" which the dispatcher treated as removal, popping baseline-set keys; fixed by switching baseline-side "no contribution" to `""` while keeping `None`-removal only on `_sensorium_ext_no_memories_flag_override`. 25 focused tests pass at `tests/test_ad723_sensorium_dispatch.py` (over the ~14 target): types (3 — path values are strings, frozen dataclass, default fields); registry shape (2 — all entries are `SensoriumEntry`, all method names resolve on `CognitiveAgent` — phantom-API guard); path coherence (4 — avatar not in WR, intent-self-tag not in WR, `_build_dm_self_monitoring` is WR-only not DM, situation entries chain-only); dispatcher core (9 — sync returns dict, empty-paths skip, sync rejects async with explicit RuntimeError, async awaits coroutines, None signals removal, "" skipped without pop, dict multi-key with embedded None pops, exception is Tier-2 degrade, priority ordering — extensions override baseline by key); AD-646 None-removal (2 — extensions pop baseline `_no_episodic_memories` when memories present; set the flag when framing-but-no-memories); chain-baseline snapshot byte-equality (2 — fixture match + shim/dispatcher equivalence); chain extraction coverage (2 — extracted methods invocable with full observation; situation dispatch returns only situation keys); AD-722/AD-722a default-OFF guard (1 — neither avatar nor intent-self-tag key in baseline dict at default config). All 109 existing tests across `test_ad646_cognitive_baseline.py` / `test_ad646b_chain_parity.py` / `test_ad722_avatar_telemetry.py` / `test_ad722a_divergence_detector.py` / `test_ad635f_clinical_proactive_context.py` / `test_ad648_post_capability_profiles.py` pass without modification — the sync signatures of all four chain shims are preserved exactly. 2 tests in `test_ad666_sensorium.py` (`test_registry_entries_are_tuples_of_layer_and_description`, `test_registry_has_all_three_layers`) updated in place to assert the new `SensoriumEntry` dataclass shape (the registry shape change is the AD's whole point). **Architect calls (DLogs)**: (1) `MagicMock(spec=CognitiveAgent)` test pattern in AD-635f required `_build_situation_awareness` to inline its dispatch loop and resolve methods via the class (`getattr(CognitiveAgent, method_name)`) instead of `self`, so Mock-bound calls don't intercept the dispatcher path; (2) `_ext_context_parts` helper for reading `observation["_context_parts"]` was originally a `@staticmethod` but Mock-spec intercepts class attribute access too — inlined the dict read at every call site (15 callers); (3) baseline-side methods return `""` (no contribution) instead of `None`; only `_sensorium_ext_no_memories_flag_override` keeps `None`-as-removal semantic, matching the AD-646 contract documented in §6.4 of the prompt. Pre-commit deletion sanity: 428 deletions in `cognitive_agent.py` are the inline-block extractions (~150 from baseline + ~140 from extensions + ~100 from situation + 16 from old registry literal = ~406; remaining ~22 are reshuffled imports / docstrings). Anticipated by the prompt; legitimate refactor. Full gate: 13196 passed, 20 skipped, 4 pre-existing flakes (test_callsign_routing × 3, test_ad719_chat_fanout × 1) verified against `2e7eb52` via `git stash`. No hard-stops triggered: no architectural change required, no phantom in implementation, no scope creep beyond the producer-side reframe.

AD-569a (Frame Diversity), AD-569b (Synthesis Detection), AD-569c (Cross-Department Trigger), AD-569e (Anchor-Grounded Emergence) shipped via the AD-569 main entry — `BehavioralSnapshot` carries all five metric field groups and `BehavioralMetricsEngine` updates them on Dream Step 13. AD-569d shipped via AD-583f/g satisfaction — `ObservableStateVerifier` populates `convergence_correctness_rate` (decisions-era-4-evolution.md:3219). AD-569g v1 builds in this wave: HXI-only `BehavioralMetricsPanel` floating dashboard surfaces the five behavioral metrics as 2-column tiles with composite `behavioral_quality_score` header, pure-SVG sparklines (raw 0-100 viewBox math, no `getBoundingClientRect`), department chips on the Frame Diversity tile, em-dash for null `convergence_correctness_rate`, ESC-to-close, and footer note "Facet breakdown lands with AD-569f". Backend zero-touch — only consumes `/api/behavioral-metrics` and `/api/behavioral-metrics/history` (both shipped under AD-569 at `routers/system.py:299-326`). Store key `behavioralMetricsOpen` (full name, no abbreviation), inline `METRICS` toggle at `top:12 left:270` to the right of `NOTEBOOKS`. AD-569f deferred with explicit forcing function — research-grade psychometric layer (G-study/D-study, ICC, r_wg, MTMM, variance decomposition) with no present-day consumer; AD-583f/g satisfied 569d without it; reframes when first qualification probe requires variance decomposition. 12 focused vitest tests pass at `ui/src/__tests__/BehavioralMetricsPanel.test.tsx` (over 9 floor by 3): renders nothing when closed; opens and fetches both endpoints; renders all five metric tiles; composite quality score in header; empty state for `not_available`; empty state for `no_data`; em-dash for null convergence_correctness; close button closes panel; error state on fetch reject; sparkline polyline with 3+ history points; dashed baseline when history empty; refresh button re-invokes fetches. Vitest delta +12 (279 → 291 passing); single pre-existing WardRoomDmSync failure unchanged. Pytest collection 11498 unchanged (Δ = 0; backend verify-only). HXI Design Principles compliance: zero emoji (Unicode glyphs `×` close, `↻` refresh only); SVG strokes `1.5` rounded; amber/teal/violet/green/gold trust spectrum across the five tiles. No commercial language in panel copy, prompt, dispatch, or roadmap. Files modified: `ui/src/store/types.ts` (+`BehavioralSnapshot`), `ui/src/store/useStore.ts` (+state shape, +actions interface, +initial state, +three actions), `ui/src/App.tsx` (+import, +`BehavioralMetricsToggle`, +mount points). Files added: `ui/src/components/BehavioralMetricsPanel.tsx`, `ui/src/__tests__/BehavioralMetricsPanel.test.tsx`. No `src/probos/` modifications. No new pytest files.

AD-686b v1 CLOSED. Oracle Owns SemanticKnowledgeLayer Write-Path (GH issue #414, Wave 50). Closes the AD-686 v1 (Wave 36) read/write asymmetry by completing the second half of Oracle's Tier-5 ownership. New `OracleService.write_semantic(kind, /, **fields) -> bool` is a keyword-dispatcher that routes one record to the matching `SemanticKnowledgeLayer.index_<kind>` method (five supported kinds: `agent`/`skill`/`workflow`/`qa_report`/`event`). Tier-2 log-and-degrade: returns `False` (and logs at debug) when the semantic layer is not attached; returns `False` (and logs at warning) when the kind is unknown; wraps the delegation in try/except so any layer-side failure logs at warning and returns `False` without propagating. Returns `True` only when the underlying `await layer.index_<kind>(**fields)` completes successfully. Five external write-path call sites now route through the Oracle: `runtime.py:2508` (designed-agent `index_agent`), `runtime.py:3309` (skill `index_skill`), `runtime.py:3358` (QA report `index_qa_report`), `self_mod_manager.py:142` (patched-agent `index_agent`), `routers/chat.py:419` (chat-driven design `index_agent`). Each migrated site drops its `if self._semantic_layer:` gate and inline try/except — Oracle's dispatcher provides those guarantees centrally. `routers/chat.py` and `self_mod_manager.py` use the Wave 36 `getattr(rt, "oracle", None) or getattr(rt, "_oracle_service", None)` fallback chain to tolerate stub runtimes in older test rigs. **What this AD does NOT change** (out of scope by design): no deletion of `SemanticKnowledgeLayer` (Oracle delegates to it; internal `reindex_from_store` at `semantic.py:360/376/397/413` still calls `self.index_*` directly); no deletion of `runtime._semantic_layer` attribute (`agents/introspect.py:764` still uses it as a read-path fallback when `runtime.oracle` is missing on a stub; `runtime.py:2973-2974` calls `_semantic_layer.stats()` for the system-status panel — no Oracle stats surface today); no migration of `index_workflow`/`index_event` (no external callers at HEAD; dispatcher ships those kinds for completeness); no new EventType, no new Pydantic config, no new module, no new public attribute on runtime; no write-path classification gating (AD-692 territory and applies to KnowledgeEdge, not the semantic feed); no deferred-write queue, no rate-limit, no audit emission. **Architect calls (DLogs):** (1) `write_semantic(kind, /, **fields)` instead of Captain's `(entries, *, collection)` shape — SemanticKnowledgeLayer has no uniform `add()` method; five typed methods, five distinct kwarg sets; forcing dict-packing would regress type safety; keyword dispatch by kind is the closest faithful mirror. (2) `index_workflow`/`index_event` ship in the dispatcher with no migration site — both surfaces are real (schema includes `workflows` and `events` collections) and only invoked internally by `reindex_from_store` at HEAD; tests #4 + #6 exercise them via Oracle for completeness. (3) `SemanticKnowledgeLayer` cannot be made internal in v1 — two non-test external consumers remain after this AD's 5 migrations: `introspect.py:764` read-path fallback for stub runtimes, and `runtime.py:2974` `_semantic_layer.stats()` for `/system` shell command stats panel; rename deferred to AD-686c once `OracleService.semantic_stats()` ships. (4) `bool` not `int` — `routers/chat.py` consumes the return as a bool flag; single-record dispatcher has no aggregation. (5) Migration sites drop `if self._semantic_layer:` guard + try/except — Oracle handles None + exceptions internally. (6) `self_mod_manager.py` keeps its `_semantic_layer` ctor field — cleanup is AD-686c territory; v1 stays narrow on call sites. 12 focused tests pass at `tests/test_ad686b_oracle_write_semantic.py` (over 10 floor by 2): method shape (async, present, returns bool); `agent` kind delegates with kwargs forwarded byte-for-byte; `skill` kind delegates; `workflow` kind delegates (no migration site); `qa_report` kind delegates; `event` kind delegates (no migration site); `None` layer returns `False` + debug log + does NOT raise; unknown kind returns `False` + warning + does NOT call layer (uses `MagicMock(spec=[])` so auto-attributes don't satisfy getattr lookup); delegation exception (RuntimeError from `layer.index_agent`) caught → `False` + warning, never propagates; late-bind via `attach_semantic_layer` (False before, True after); `SemanticKnowledgeLayer.index_*` write methods unchanged backward-compat lock (asserts presence by name + async-callable shape across all five); static migration lock asserting the 3 migrated source files contain `oracle.write_semantic(` AND zero direct `_semantic_layer.index_*(` writes remain (catches future refactors that silently revert one of the five sites). All 11 existing AD-686 v1 read-path tests at `tests/test_ad686_oracle_semantic_tier.py` continue to pass without modification. Phantom-API pre-check on the prompt body: 15 candidates ALL FPs — 14× `OracleService.write_semantic` intra-prompt-introduction (same FP class as Waves 27-49) + 1× `OracleService.semantic_stats` DLog #3 prose (hypothetical AD-686c surface, not asserted to exist); 0 NEW phantoms. **Builder drift-fix during build**: test #8 (`test_write_semantic_unknown_kind_returns_false`) initially failed with `TypeError: object MagicMock can't be used in 'await' expression` because `MagicMock()` auto-creates attributes — `getattr(layer, "index_nonexistent", None)` returned a MagicMock, not None, then awaiting the MagicMock raised TypeError, which the `except Exception:` caught as a delegation failure (logging "delegation failed" instead of "unknown kind"). Fix: `MagicMock(spec=[])` constrains the mock so it has zero attributes, restoring the intended unknown-kind path. Pure test-fixture defect; no shipping-code change. Lesson: when testing a getattr-by-name dispatcher, the mock layer must use `spec=[]` (or a non-MagicMock class) to make missing attributes genuinely missing. Full gate: 11170 passed, 16 skipped (delta exactly +12 vs Wave 49 baseline 11158 — exact target hit). Pre-commit deletion sanity: max ~16 deletions any single file (line-level inline-try/except blocks replaced with single-line awaits at the 5 migration sites). All other files small additive deltas. Well below 200-line surprise-deletion threshold. No hard-stops triggered: no architectural change required, no phantom in implementation, no scope creep. Closes GH issue #414.

AD-647c v1 CLOSED. Process Chains Bills/Watch Bill Integration (GH issue #405, Wave 49). Bridges process chains to AD-618 Bills + AD-595 Watch Bill: `ProcessChainStep` gains optional `bill_step_id` and `assigned_role` fields (both default `""`), `ProcessChainExecutor` accepts `bill_runtime=None` ctor kwarg + four tier-2 log-and-degrade helpers (`_resolve_bill_instance` resolves active `BillInstance` once at chain start via `runtime.bill_runtime.get_instance(context['bill_instance_id'])` with three guards: bill_runtime present AND context.bill_instance_id present AND lookup succeeds; `_inject_resolved_agent` reads `BillInstance.role_assignments[step.assigned_role].agent_id` and injects as `_resolved_agent_id_<step.name>` into running context; `_record_bill_step_completion` calls `bill_runtime.complete_step(instance.id, step.bill_step_id, result=step_output)` on success; `_record_bill_step_failure` calls `bill_runtime.fail_step(instance.id, step.bill_step_id, error=...)` on handler exception). Every bill_runtime call wrapped in try/except→logger.warning so bill-side errors NEVER propagate up into chain execution — chain success is the source of truth, Bills tracking is a secondary observability surface. New `ProcessChainRegistry.register_bill_chain(bill_definition, chain_definition)` method validates every chain step's non-empty `bill_step_id` against the bill's `BillStep.id` set; mismatch → `ValueError("AD-647c: chain '<name>' references unknown bill step ids in bill '<slug>': <step.name>-><bsid>, ...")` and chain is NOT registered (fail-fast). Empty bill_step_id values are permitted and skip validation. New CONSULT step kind appended to `ProcessChainStepKind` enum (semantic label only — executor already awaits async handlers via `await step.handler(running)`, so human-in-the-loop / cross-agent consultation handlers that internally await asyncio.Event/ward-room-thread/ConsultationWorkspace decisions work today). Suspend/resume across process restart deferred to placeholder AD-647d. New public property `runtime.bill_runtime` mirrors `runtime.billet_registry` shape — closes the Wave 5 conv #1 hole that AD-635 documented retrospectively for `_emergent_detector`; ProcessChainExecutor uses this to record chain-step lifecycle against BillInstance steps when chains are bill-bound. **What this AD does NOT change** (out of scope by design): no NATS coupling (deferred AD-641g, separate large issue #403); no LLM step templates (`prompt_template_id` still reserved/rejected at construction); no parallel/conditional/rollback steps; no CONSULT suspend-and-resume across process restart (deferred AD-647d, no GH issue v1; documented in module docstring + DLog only); no Scout migration to bill-bound (Scout reports are not tied to a Bill); no HXI surface for chain↔bill correlation; no new EventType / journal entries; no `runtime._bill_runtime` deletion (kept for adoption-site compatibility). **Architect calls (DLogs):** (1) Field renamed `bill_step_id` not `bill_id` — `bill_id` is overloaded across the Bills surface (`BillDefinition.bill` is the slug; `BillInstance.bill_id` is the slug too); chain step's field maps to `BillStep.id` (the step id WITHIN a bill). The bill-instance correlation is supplied by the caller via `context['bill_instance_id']` at run-time — orthogonal field, not on the step. (2) CONSULT = enum value only, NO executor change — existing executor at `process_chains.py:131` already runs `await step.handler(running)` which natively supports `async def __call__` handlers that internally await any async resource. True suspend-and-resume across process restart needs checkpointing infrastructure → out of scope. (3) `runtime.bill_runtime` public property added — 1-line property mirroring `runtime.billet_registry` shape (`runtime.py:985`); cleaner than re-creating AD-635's `getattr(rt, '_bill_runtime', None)` Demeter exception. (4) Three-guard defense for bill recording — bill_step_id != "" AND context.bill_instance_id present AND bill_runtime is not None; all three required to fire side-effects. (5) Bill recording is tier-2 log-and-degrade — every `bill_runtime.{get_instance, complete_step, fail_step}` call wrapped in try/except; chain success preserved on bill-side error. (6) `register_bill_chain` fail-fast — mismatched `bill_step_id` raises `ValueError` and chain is NOT registered. (7) Unresolved `assigned_role` — log-and-degrade; handler still runs, just no `_resolved_agent_id_<step>` injected (executor does NOT synthesize a fall-back identity; handler decides). 13 focused tests pass at `tests/test_ad647c_bills_integration.py` (over 10 floor by 3): `bill_step_id`/`assigned_role` field acceptance; `CONSULT` enum value (`.value == "consult"`); executor without `bill_runtime` runs identically to v1 (bill_step_id ignored); executor with `bill_runtime` but no `context.bill_instance_id` skips recording (`get_instance.assert_not_called()`); executor records `complete_step("i1", "bstep_alpha", result={"out": 42})` on success; executor records `fail_step("i1", "bstep_beta", error="RuntimeError: nope")` on handler exception (and `complete_step.assert_not_called()`); tier-2 log-and-degrade preserves chain success when `bill_runtime.complete_step.side_effect = RuntimeError("bill-side bug")` (output dict intact, WARNING captured); `assigned_role="oncall"` injects `_resolved_agent_id_s1="agent-007"` from `BillInstance.role_assignments`; unresolved role logs WARNING ("has no holder") and `_resolved_agent_id_s1` not in context; `register_bill_chain` happy path (chain registered + retrievable); `register_bill_chain` rejects mismatched bill_step_ids with `ValueError("unknown bill step ids")` and chain NOT registered (`get_chain("bad") is None`); empty `bill_step_id` values skip validation. All 8 existing AD-647 v1 tests + 12 existing AD-647b v1 tests continue to pass unchanged — new fields default to `""`, new ctor kwarg defaults to `None`, no call site changes required. Phantom-API pre-check: 6 candidates ALL FPs (1 stdlib `SimpleNamespace` + 3 `ProcessChainRegistry.register_bill_chain` method_phantom + 2 `ProcessChainStep.bill_step_id`/`assigned_role` field_phantom — same intra-prompt-introduction FP class as Waves 27–48). 0 NEW phantoms. Full gate: 11158 passed, 16 skipped (delta +13 vs Wave 48 baseline 11145 — over the +12 acceptance-criteria target by 1). Pre-commit deletion sanity: max ~3 deletions any single file (additive-only changes — enum +1 value, ProcessChainStep +2 fields, ProcessChainExecutor +1 ctor kwarg + 4 helpers + run() rewrite, ProcessChainRegistry +1 method, runtime.py +1 public property, new test file). Well below 200-line surprise-deletion threshold. No hard-stops triggered: no architectural change required, no phantom in implementation, no scope creep. Closes GH issue #405.

AD-647b v1 CLOSED. Process Chain Registry + BF-209 Bypass Removal (GH issue #404, Wave 48). Closes the AD-647 v1 hardcoded-chain gap by introducing a runtime catalog of `ProcessChainDefinition` and generalizing the BF-209 communication-chain bypass into a declarative class-attribute hook on `CognitiveAgent`. Three converging components shipped in one OSS v1: (1) **`ProcessChainRegistry`** — new class in `cognitive/process_chains.py` keyed by `chain_id = definition.name`. Public API: `register_chain(definition)` replaces + WARNs on duplicate (mirrors `ToolRegistry.register` at `tools/registry.py:113`), `get_chain(chain_id) -> ProcessChainDefinition | None` (Demeter-friendly None-on-miss), `list_chains() -> list[str]` sorted, `unregister_chain(chain_id) -> bool` (False on absent, no exception). (2) **`process_chain_id` base-class hook** — new class attribute `process_chain_id: str | None = None` on `CognitiveAgent`. `_should_activate_chain` Gate 0 short-circuits to False when `self.process_chain_id is not None` AND observation is `proactive_think` with `params.duty.duty_id == self.process_chain_id`. This generalizes BF-209 from a per-agent override into a declarative class attribute. (3) **Scout migration** — four `_scout_step_*` methods promoted from bound instance methods (Wave 34 shape — handlers reached `self._reports_dir`/`self._pending_seen_repos`/`self._deliver_discord`/`self.id` via implicit self) to **module-level functions** in `scout.py` that read `_agent` from `ctx["_agent"]` then access per-instance state through the agent reference. `SCOUT_REPORT_CHAIN: ProcessChainDefinition` becomes a static module-level constant immediately above `class ScoutAgent`. `ScoutAgent.process_chain_id = "scout_report"`. The BF-209 `_should_activate_chain` override is REMOVED — Method Resolution Order now resolves `ScoutAgent._should_activate_chain` to `CognitiveAgent._should_activate_chain` (test #10 asserts function-object identity). `act()` chain construction replaced by `runtime.process_chain_registry.get_chain(self.process_chain_id)` with module-level `SCOUT_REPORT_CHAIN` fallback + WARNING when registry is unavailable. `_deliver_discord` stays as a bound instance method (reads `self._runtime`/`self.config`/`self.id` at call time — not a step handler). (4) **Pydantic config + finalize wirer** — new `ProcessChainRegistryConfig(enabled=True)` adjacent to `ConsultationWorkspaceConfig` at `config.py:1904`; wired on `SystemConfig.process_chain_registry` adjacent to `consultation_workspaces` at `:2260`. Default-True is intentional + documented in config docstring — registry construction cost is one empty dict + one `register_chain(SCOUT_REPORT_CHAIN)` call at boot, and Scout's `act()` depends on the registry being present (module-level fallback is a defensive belt). New sync `_wire_process_chain_registry(*, runtime, config) -> bool` wirer in `startup/finalize.py` adjacent to `_wire_consultation_workspaces` constructs the registry, registers `SCOUT_REPORT_CHAIN`, sets `runtime.process_chain_registry` (collision-free greenfield — verified 0 hits at HEAD before edit), invoked from `finalize_startup` immediately before `_wire_consultation_workspaces`. (5) **Test fixture amendment** — `test_scout_act_runs_through_process_chain` (`test_ad647_process_chains.py:152`) replaced `agent._runtime = None` with a `SimpleNamespace(process_chain_registry=ProcessChainRegistry())` populated with `SCOUT_REPORT_CHAIN` — Scout's `act()` now finds the chain via the runtime registry path (the production path) instead of the module-level fallback (which would log a WARNING per test invocation). **What this AD does NOT change** (out of scope by design): no NATS / Bills / Watch Bill integration (AD-647c, #405); no parallel / conditional / rollback steps (v1 stays sequential per AD-647 v1 invariant); no LLM-template step handlers (`prompt_template_id` field on `ProcessChainStep` remains rejected at construction); no registry persistence (in-memory only); no HXI surface (`list_chains()` not exposed via REST or shell); no `_deliver_discord` refactor (stays bound; only the four step methods move to module level); no `BaseAgent` change (`process_chain_id` lives on `CognitiveAgent` — Scout's actual parent; future non-cognitive agents would promote via AD-647d). **Architect call (DLog #1) — module-level handlers**: registry stores definitions, not factories — handlers cannot be per-instance bound. Refactor is mechanical: `self.X` → `agent = ctx["_agent"]` then `agent.X`. This is the correct long-term shape. **Architect call (DLog #2) — hook lives on `_should_activate_chain`, NOT default `act()`**: `CognitiveAgent.act()` already has substantial logic (chain-result aggregation, validation) that we don't want to fork. The cleaner hook is on `_should_activate_chain`, which is exactly what BF-209 overrides. **Architect call (DLog #3) — duplicate-registration semantics**: replace + WARN matches `ToolRegistry.register`; more useful for hot-reload / test isolation than rejection. **Architect call (DLog #4) — registry never raises on miss**: `get_chain(unknown)` returns None, `unregister_chain(unknown)` returns False. Demeter-friendly; caller decides fallback. **Architect call (DLog #5) — `enabled=True` default**: same precedent as `KnowledgeEdgesConfig` / `EdgeBackfillConfig` / `ConsultationWorkspaceConfig`. Disabling means Scout's `act()` falls back to the module-level constant and logs WARNING per invocation — operator-visible degradation rather than silent breakage. **Architect call (DLog #6) — backward-compat invariant**: all 8 existing `tests/test_ad647_process_chains.py` tests continue to pass after the Section 6 amendment. 12 focused tests pass at `tests/test_ad647b_chain_registry.py` (over the 10 floor by 2): registry register+get+list happy path; registry get-unknown returns None; registry unregister returns True when present + clears entry; registry unregister-unknown returns False; registry duplicate registration replaces with WARNING captured via `caplog` + new instance returned by object-identity check; `_should_activate_chain` returns False when `process_chain_id` matches duty (with FakeExecutor.enabled=True); falls through to gates 1+2 when `process_chain_id` is None (proactive_think + executor enabled → True); falls through when `process_chain_id` set but `duty_id` mismatches; `ScoutAgent.process_chain_id == "scout_report"` class attribute assertion; `ScoutAgent._should_activate_chain is CognitiveAgent._should_activate_chain` function-object identity (BF-209 override removed); `_scout_step_enrich_and_filter` reads agent from `ctx["_agent"]` correctly (filter_findings + agent._last_findings updated); finalize wirer constructs registry and registers SCOUT_REPORT_CHAIN against a real `SystemConfig()` + SimpleNamespace runtime, asserts `list_chains() == ["scout_report"]` and the registered chain's first step is `parse_and_mark_seen`. Phantom-API pre-check on this prompt and on the touched source files: clean (no NEW phantoms; collision-free greenfield for `runtime.process_chain_registry` and `process_chain_id` class attribute verified pre-build). Full gate: 11144 passed, 16 skipped (delta +12 vs Wave 47 baseline 11132 — exact target hit). 1 known xdist flake (`test_knowledge_store::test_auto_commit_after_debounce` — passes serial in 7.41s; same Waves 8/14/15/16/19/22/27/30/31/32/33/37/41/42 environmental git-debounce pattern; unrelated to this AD). Pre-commit deletion sanity check: max ~110 deletions in `scout.py` (4 bound `_scout_step_*` methods removed — content moved to module level above the class). All other files small line-level deltas: `cognitive_agent.py` +18/-3 (class attribute + Gate 0 logic), `process_chains.py` +35/-0 (registry append-only), `config.py` +14/-0 (config model + field), `finalize.py` +25/-0 (wirer + invoke), `test_ad647_process_chains.py` +7/-1 (fixture amendment), new `test_ad647b_chain_registry.py` +185/-0. Well below 200-line surprise-deletion threshold per file in tracker context. No hard-stops triggered: no architectural change required, no phantom in implementation, no scope creep. Closes GH issue #404.

AD-685d v1 CLOSED. Phantom-API Pre-Check Dataclass/Pydantic Field-Name Validation (GH issue #407, Wave 47). Fourth capability of `scripts/phantom-api-precheck.ps1` extending AD-685 (kwarg-name) + AD-685b (method-name) + AD-685c (kwarg type-shape) with dataclass/Pydantic field-name validation: catches `meta.totel_ops` attribute typos and `AgentMeta(succes_count=5)` constructor kwarg typos against transitive field sets, plus child fields shadowing parent `@property` or method as `property_field_collision`. New 5th cache `_CLASS_FIELDS_CACHE` and `build_class_field_index()` AST walker indexes `@dataclass`-decorated and `BaseModel`-subclass classes → `{fields, parents, properties, methods, kind}`. `_resolve_transitive_fields()` cycle-safe recursive parent walk. `find_field_phantoms()` detects two patterns: constructor kwargs via `_CTOR_RE` (CamelCase + paren block, AST-parsed for kwarg names) and attribute access via `_ATTR_RE` (`obj.field` not followed by `(`); skips chained access like `runtime.X.field` and the `runtime` receiver itself per AD-685b precedent; only flags Pattern B (`var = MyClass(...)`)-resolvable receivers. `find_property_field_collisions()` walks each indexed class's transitive parents and flags every (child_field, parent_property) and (child_field, parent_method) collision. New categories: `field_phantom` (with `access_kind=constructor|attribute`, `valid_fields[:20]`) and `property_field_collision` (with `kind=property|method`, `child`, `parent`, `name`). PowerShell wrapper extends dispatch with two new `elseif` branches. ClassVar-annotated names excluded from fields per Python semantics; private-prefix fields skipped (ProbOS narrow-private-access convention); plain (non-dataclass non-Pydantic) classes recorded ONLY when they declare properties or methods (supports parent-side collision lookup without flagging non-dataclass typos). 12 focused tests pass at `tests/test_ad685d_phantom_field_name.py` (over 10 floor by 2): dataclass known-field happy + unknown attribute flagged + unknown ctor kwarg flagged; Pydantic known-field happy + unknown field flagged; property/field collision flagged; method/field collision flagged; inherited fields recognized via transitive walk; var-no-resolve silent skip; output category strings exact; ClassVar excluded; subprocess self-test on prompt body returns exit 0 with FPs constrained to fixture class names. Existing 35 phantom-API pre-check tests (`test_phantom_api_precheck_kwargs.py` + `test_phantom_api_precheck_method_calls.py` + `test_ad685c_phantom_type_shape.py`) continue to pass — record-shape stable. Backward compat preserved: AD-685 records keep no `category` field; AD-685b/c records unchanged. **What this AD does NOT change** (out of scope by design): no TypedDict field validation (defer AD-685e); no runtime introspection (strictly AST-static — fields injected via metaclass / `__init_subclass__` are invisible); no per-instance type narrowing across function boundaries (Pattern B resolves within prompt body only); no constructor kwarg type-shape on dataclass fields (AD-685c handles type-shape against function annotations; extending to dataclass field annotations is AD-685e candidate); no NamedTuple / attrs / msgspec; no exit-code semantics change; no new directory layout (helper grew ~280 lines to ~1280 total). Helper passes JSON-safety check inherited from AD-685c (no AST nodes leak to output). Full gate: 11134 passed, 15 skipped (delta +12 vs Wave 46 baseline 11122 — exact target hit). Pre-commit deletion sanity: max ~3 deletions any single file (additive-only — wrapper elseif inserted as new branch, helper additions all append, new test file). No hard-stops triggered: no architectural change required, no phantom in implementation (verify-first pre-check at draft confirmed all 8 anchors at HEAD `893f29b`: `TypeShape`/`ValueShape` line numbers, `find_type_shape_phantoms` insertion site, `_RUNTIME_CONFLICTS_CACHE` cache slot, `build_class_method_index` insertion anchor, type_shape_mismatch wrapper dispatch, `@dataclass` and `class.*BaseModel` patterns in src). Closes GH issue #407.

 Phantom-API Pre-Check Type-Shape Validation (GH issue #406, Wave 46). Third capability of `scripts/phantom-api-precheck.ps1` extends AD-685 v1 (kwarg-name shape) + AD-685b v1 (method-name on resolved class) with kwarg-value type-shape validation: when a prompt asserts `obj.method(name=42)` and `name: str` is the live signature annotation, flag as `type_shape_mismatch`. Single-file extension to `scripts/phantom_api_ast_helper.py` (NO new package; verified at draft time the Captain spec referenced `scripts/phantom-api-precheck/` directory but reality is one helper file + one wrapper). Implementation: (1) `_collect_param_annotations(func)` parallel collector mirroring `_collect_param_names()` for positional/kwonly/*args/**kwargs categories; `build_index()` records `param_annotations: dict[str, ast.AST]` alongside the existing `params: list[str]` in the same single AST walk (zero extra file I/O — meets <2x perf bound trivially). (2) Slotted `TypeShape` class with `literal_types: frozenset[str]`, `allow_none: bool`, `container: str | None`, `element_shapes: tuple[TypeShape, ...]`, `unknown: bool` + `is_skippable()` predicate. `_annotation_to_type_shape(node)` pure-AST resolver handles primitives (str/int/float/bool/bytes), `Optional[X]`, `X | None`, `None | X`, `Union[A, B, ...]`, `list[T]` / `dict[K, V]` / `tuple[T, ...]` / `tuple[T1, T2]` / `set[T]` / `frozenset[T]` plus bare `list`/`dict`/`tuple`/`set` containers (permissive at element level when no type-parameter); any unrecognised class name (e.g. `KnowledgeEdge`) yields `unknown=True` which the validator treats as SKIP. Helpers `_iter_tuple_elts`, `_flatten_bitor`, `_union_shape` factor common subscript/BitOr handling. (3) Slotted `ValueShape` + `_value_to_shape(node)` pure-AST classifier handles `ast.Constant` for str/int/float/bool/None (with bytes returning `None` per Captain spec — "skipped on bytes/bytes-like — cannot resolve"), `ast.List`/`ast.Set`/`ast.Tuple`/`ast.Dict` containers (dict encodes keys+values into one element_shapes tuple); `ast.Name`/`ast.Call`/`ast.Attribute`/anything else returns `None` (silent skip). `_value_matches_shape(value, shape)` compatibility check is conservative: skippable shapes return True (never flag); `None` matches iff `allow_none`; primitive matches iff `value.primitive in shape.literal_types`; `bool` value also matches `int` annotation (Python semantics — bool IS-A int); container value matches iff container kind matches AND every element value-shape matches the corresponding element type-shape (homogeneous for list/set/`tuple[T, ...]`, positional for `tuple[T1, T2, T3]`, key/value for dict); empty container matches any container of the same kind (no element evidence to refute); annotation with `unknown=True` skips (returns True; never flag). (4) `find_type_shape_phantoms(body, index)` scans `_CALL_RE` matches the same way as `find_kwarg_phantoms` — skips noisy receivers/methods, skips `**kwargs`-accepting candidates (kwarg name is permissively accepted; annotation is unknown), re-parses the call's kwarg block as `ast.parse(f"_f({kwarg_block})", mode="eval")` to get value ASTs, then for each keyword whose name is accepted by the live signatures collects the union of param-annotation shapes across matching candidates. Flag iff (a) at least one candidate accepts the kwarg AND has an annotation for it, AND (b) NONE of the applicable shapes match the value. If ANY candidate has the kwarg but no annotation → permissive across all candidates → no flag. Emits `category="type_shape_mismatch"` records with `value_type` (primitive name or container kind label), `expected_types` (sorted union of literal_types across applicable shapes), `kwarg`, and a JSON-safe candidate slice (see point 5). (5) `_jsonable_candidate(c)` projects candidate dicts to a JSON-serializable subset by stripping `param_annotations` (AST nodes are not JSON-serializable). Both `find_kwarg_phantoms()` and `find_type_shape_phantoms()` use this when emitting `candidates: candidates[:5]`; the existing tests `test_phantom_api_precheck_kwargs.py` rely on the existing JSON shape and would break otherwise. (6) `main()` extends the dispatch list: `phantoms = kwarg_phantoms + method_phantoms + type_shape_phantoms`. PowerShell wrapper `scripts/phantom-api-precheck.ps1` adds a third `elseif ($p.category -eq 'type_shape_mismatch')` branch in the helper-output dispatch block; renders as `<method>(<kwarg>=<<value_type>> -> expected <<expected>|<expected>>)` with `<unknown>` fallback when no expected literal types resolved (e.g. annotation was a container only). No exit-code semantics change — type-shape phantoms flow into the same `phantoms` list and contribute to non-zero exit per existing rule. Backward compat preserved: existing AD-685 v1 kwarg-phantom records (no `category` field) and AD-685b method-phantom records (`category="method_phantom"`) ship unchanged in shape. **Architect call (DLog #1) — single-file extension preserved**: Captain's spec implied a `scripts/phantom-api-precheck/` directory; verify-first at draft confirmed reality at HEAD `c33c38d` is one helper file + one PowerShell wrapper. Splitting into a package would be a separate refactor AD; v1 stays single-file (helper grew ~280 lines). **Architect call (DLog #2) — when ANY candidate accepts the kwarg but has no annotation, the validator skips this kwarg**: avoids false positives against legacy unannotated APIs. Only when EVERY applicable candidate has an annotation does the validator flag. **Architect call (DLog #3) — bool matches int annotation**: Python's bool IS-A int (`isinstance(True, int) == True`); flagging `f(count=True)` against `def f(count: int)` would be a false positive. **Architect call (DLog #4) — empty container is permissive**: an empty list/dict/tuple/set has no element evidence to refute, so `f(tags=[])` matches both `list[str]` and `list[int]`. **Architect call (DLog #5) — bytes literal returns None (silent skip)**: bytes-like content cannot be reasoned about statically without runtime context. **Architect call (DLog #6) — unknown class annotation skips entirely**: `def w(item: KnowledgeEdge)` plus `obj.w(item=42)` does NOT flag because the validator cannot statically resolve KnowledgeEdge's structural shape. AD-685d (Wave 47) adds dataclass / Pydantic field-name validation for those cases. **Builder drift-fix during build**: helper crashed on first JSON serialization with `TypeError: Object of type BinOp is not JSON serializable` because `param_annotations` AST nodes leaked into the `candidates[:5]` slice in both `find_kwarg_phantoms` and `find_type_shape_phantoms` records. Added `_jsonable_candidate(c)` projection that strips `param_annotations` before serialization; both phantom emitters now route through it. This restored the 7 existing `test_phantom_api_precheck_kwargs.py` tests that broke between baseline 11107 and the helper edit (they were re-running the wrapper end-to-end via subprocess and depended on the helper not crashing). 15 focused tests pass at `tests/test_ad685c_phantom_type_shape.py` (over the 10 floor by 5): `_value_to_shape` primitives + bytes-skip + Name-skip + Attribute/Call-skip; `_annotation_to_type_shape` for primitives + unknown class + Optional + `X | None` + `None | X` + `int | str` + `Union[int, float]`; container annotations (`list[str]` shape, bare `list`, `dict[str, int]` two element shapes); match `str → str` no phantom; mismatch `int → str` flagged with value_type=int + expected=["str"]; None matches `Optional[str]` + None to `str` flagged; `list[str]` matches `["a","b"]` + `list[str]` flagged for `[1,2]`; variable reference silent skip; bytes literal silent skip; `int | str` accepts int + flags float; unknown-class annotation skipped for any value; backward-compat AD-685 kwarg phantom record shape unchanged (no `category` field); Captain-required wrapper self-test runs `pwsh ./scripts/phantom-api-precheck.ps1 prompts/ad-685c-phantom-type-shape-v1.md` via subprocess and asserts exit code in {0, 1} with stderr captured. **What this AD does NOT change** (out of scope by design): no dataclass / Pydantic field-name typo detection (AD-685d, Wave 47); no type-shape on return values (separate AD if ever needed); no runtime introspection (strictly AST-static — unknown class annotations are SKIPPED); no new directory layout (single-file helper grows; package split would be separate refactor); no exit-code semantics change; no PowerShell `$STDLIB_PREFIXES`/`$DOC_FILE_PATTERN` change. Phantom-API pre-check on the AD-685c prompt itself: 0 candidates, clean (cleanest result class — same as Waves 27/36/37/38). Full gate: 11122 passed, 15 skipped (delta +15 vs Wave 45 baseline 11107 — over the +10-12 acceptance-criteria target by 3-5; the +15 is 12 new helper-shape tests + 3 boundary tests for compatibility rules). Pre-commit deletion sanity: max ~5 deletions any single file (additive-only changes — wrapper elseif inserted as new branch, helper additions all append, new test file). No hard-stops triggered: no architectural change required, no phantom in implementation (verify-first pre-check at draft time confirmed the single-file shape + insertion anchors). Closes GH issue #406.

AD-661b + AD-661c v1 CLOSED. Ship's Records consumption + budget remainder redistribution (combo Wave 45, GH issues #412 + #413). Two converging extensions to AD-661 v1 DiagnosticContextService landed in one Builder cycle per Captain's "no trivial deferral" rule. (1) **AD-661b — Ship's Records (AD-434) tier**: `DiagnosticBundle` gains a 4th `records: list[dict]` field (default_factory=list, present in `to_dict()` with copy semantics); new `_gather_record_candidates(*, keywords)` collector reads `runtime.records_store.list_entries()` + `read_entry(path, reader_id, reader_department)` using a synthetic system reader identity (module-level `_RECORDS_READER_ID = "_diagnostic_context_system"` with empty department — naturally surfaces only ship/fleet records via RecordsStore's built-in classification gate; per-agent record authorization is deferred AD-661f). Two-phase keyword filter: title-substring first (cheap), full-content fallback when title misses. Each accepted record normalized to a flat dict (`path`/`title`/`summary_excerpt`/`classification`/`author`/`status`/`tags`); content excerpt truncated to `_RECORDS_CONTENT_EXCERPT_CHARS = 1200`; candidate list hard-capped at `_MAX_RECORDS_CANDIDATES = 30` before token-trim. Tier-2 log-and-degrade — missing `records_store` or any read failure yields `records=[]` without raising. (2) **AD-661c — budget remainder redistribution**: existing `_collect_*` collectors renamed to `_gather_*` candidate producers (no per-tier budget clip); new centralized `_fill_with_redistribution(candidates_by_tier, allocations, total_budget, redistribute) -> (filled, truncated)` does two-pass fill in `_TIER_PRIORITY = ("chain_traces", "procedures", "episodes", "records")` priority order. Pass 1 fills each tier up to its allocated budget; Pass 2 (only when `redistribute=True`) walks tiers again in priority order topping up tiers that have remaining candidates while global budget has room. `truncated=True` iff global budget is exhausted AND at least one tier still has unconsumed candidates — strictly correct in both directions (no over-claim, no under-claim). Default allocation split moved from 40/30/30 (3-tier) to 30/25/25/20 (chain_traces/procedures/episodes/records). New ctor kwargs `records_ratio=0.20` and `redistribute_remainder=True`. `DiagnosticContextConfig` Pydantic model: 4th `records_ratio: float = 0.20` field added to `field_validator` cohort; `model_validator` extended to sum all 4 ratios; new `redistribute_remainder: bool = True`. `_wire_diagnostic_context(*, runtime, config)` extended with the two new kwargs. The router (`routers/diagnostic_context.py`) is unchanged because `bundle.to_dict()` automatically includes the new `records` key. **What this AD does NOT change** (out of scope by design): no semantic search of records (still keyword-only); no per-agent records authorization (uses synthetic system reader, deferred AD-661f); no AD-692-style classification gating beyond what RecordsStore already enforces inside `read_entry`; no records-aware HXI surface; no AD-661d push notifications; no AD-661e LLM summary fallback; no new EventType, no new agent, no new pool, no new Pydantic config beyond the 2 added fields, no new module. **Architect call (DLog #1) — synthetic reader naturally yields ship/fleet only**: AD-434 RecordsStore's `read_entry(path, reader_id, reader_department)` enforces classification taxonomy (`private`/`department`/`ship`/`fleet`) inside the call. Empty `reader_department` plus a non-real reader id means private+department records are filtered out by the store itself before reaching the bundle; ship+fleet records pass through. This is the correct safe default for system-level diagnostic context. Documented in module docstring + config docstring. **Architect call (DLog #2) — AD-692 edge classification orthogonal**: `KnowledgeEdgeClassificationGate` (Wave 42) is for `KnowledgeEdge` graph data, NOT records. RecordsStore has its own per-record classification handled inside `read_entry`. NO edge-style decorator wrapping introduced here. **Architect call (DLog #3) — back-compat invariant**: When records_store is None and `redistribute_remainder=False`, chain_traces/procedures/episodes content is identical to v1. New `test_v1_backcompat_chain_procedures_episodes_unchanged` asserts the procedure-exemplar resolution path matches v1 expectations exactly under those conditions. **Builder drift-fix during build**: v1 test `test_chain_trace_keyword_filter_and_budget` (test_ad661_diagnostic_context.py:81) asserted `bundle.truncated is True` against the old per-tier hard cap; under the new redistribute=True default, the c3 row that overflowed chain_budget=600 fits in the global budget=2000 via redistribution → `truncated=False`. Architect's blanket "all 8 v1 tests pass unchanged" claim was overscoped vs the parenthetical invariant ("when records absent AND redistribution disabled"). Minimal fixture amendment: bumped c3 `error_truncated` from `"x"*6000` → `"x"*12000` so the row exceeds the GLOBAL budget rather than just chain_budget — preserves the test's intent (overflow → truncated). 13 focused tests pass at `tests/test_ad661bc_records_redistribution.py` (over the 10 floor by 3): config 4-ratio default + sum validator rejection; bundle.records frozen field + to_dict copy semantics; records gathered with title-hit + content-hit (paths sorted r1+r3); records empty when records_store=None; records empty when keyword has no match; records excerpt truncated to `_RECORDS_CONTENT_EXCERPT_CHARS`; records contribute to `total_estimated_tokens` (delta verified); redistribute=True flows under-fill to chain_traces (more rows than capped variant); truncated=False when all candidates fit; truncated=True when budget exhausted with leftover candidates; redistribution priority chain_traces fills before records; v1 backcompat (records=None + redistribute=False reproduces v1 procedure-exemplar shape exactly); module-level constants sanity. All 8 existing AD-661 v1 tests in `tests/test_ad661_diagnostic_context.py` continue to pass (back-compat invariant honored). Full gate: 11106 passed, 15 skipped (delta +13 vs Wave 44 baseline 11093 — over the +12 acceptance-criteria target by 1). 1 known xdist flake (`test_dreaming.py::test_nl_to_dream_cycle_changes_weights` — same Waves 23/27/30/31/32/33/39/41 environmental pattern; passes serial; unrelated to this AD). Pre-commit deletion sanity: max ~110 deletions in diagnostic_context.py (collector rename `_collect_*` → `_gather_*` is line-level rewrite); other files small line-level deltas (config.py +30/-15, finalize.py +2, test_ad661 +5/-1, new test file +280 ins / 0 del). Well below 200-line surprise-deletion threshold per file in tracker context. No hard-stops triggered: no per-agent records auth (deferred AD-661f), no semantic record search (deferred), no edge-style classification gating (orthogonal AD-692), no scope creep, no phantoms in implementation (verify-first pre-check at draft time confirmed all 11 anchors at HEAD `3c44903`: `DiagnosticBundle` frozen dc + `_collect_*` v1 collectors + `DiagnosticContextConfig` 3-ratio + `RecordsStore.list_entries`/`read_entry` + `runtime.records_store` property + `_wire_diagnostic_context` + `routers/diagnostic_context.py` + conftest `_records_store=None`). Closes GH issues #412 + #413.

AD-594a v1 CLOSED. Consultation Workspace substrate (GH issue #160, Wave 44). Session-scoped shared workspace in Ship's Records for multi-agent advisory consultations — substrate-only ship; sibling ADs AD-594b (#161 consultation primitive), AD-594c (#162 parallel execution dispatch), AD-594d (#163 delivery pipeline) are LEGITIMATELY out of scope and remain open. Three converging components landed in one OSS v1: (1) **`RecordsStore` raw-file surfaces** — three new public async methods (`write_workspace_file(author, path, content, message)`, `read_workspace_file(path) -> str | None`, `append_workspace_file(author, path, content, message)`) inserted immediately before `_parse_document` at `records_store.py:881`; bypass YAML frontmatter coercion (workspace files are YAML/Markdown of varied shapes — manifest.yaml, journal.md, delivery.yaml, plan_v{N}.md, advisory/{agent}_{ts}.md, workitems/wi_{id}.yaml — frontmatter wrapper is wrong shape); reuse existing `_safe_path` traversal protection + `_commit` git plumbing; `consultations` appended to `_SUBDIRS` tuple (mirrors `bills` AD-618a precedent) so init creates the top-level subdir. (2) **`src/probos/consultation/` package** (NEW) — five modules: `inputs.py` ships `InputProcessor` `Protocol` seam (`process(filename: str, content: bytes) -> tuple[str, bytes]`) + `PassthroughTextProcessor` v1 default impl + `build_input_processor(name)` factory with log-and-degrade fallback (Northstar II Transporter Pattern integration point — PDF/image/audio processors plug in here under separate AD; today's `cognitive/builder.py` Transporter is unrelated builder code-chunk decomposition); `refs.py` ships pure-string `parse_workspace_refs(text) -> list[WorkspaceRef]` regex extractor + `render_workspace_refs_md(text, *, base_url="/api/consultations") -> str` markdown-link substituter (HXI integration of `[workspace:<id>/<path>]` refs intentionally out of v1 — `MessageStore.create_post()` body wrapper is consumer-side task); `templates.py` ships `ArtifactType` `(str, Enum)` (5 values: ADVISORY_REPORT/PLAN_DOCUMENT/WORK_ITEM_SPEC/SUPPORTING_DATA/DECISION_RECORD) + 5 pure markdown-skeleton render functions (`render_advisory_report`/`render_plan_document`/`render_work_item_spec`/`render_supporting_data`/`render_decision_record`) + 3 consultation templates registered in `TEMPLATES: dict[str, Callable[[], str]]` (`security_review`/`technical_design`/`incident_response`) each returning a `plan_v1.md` skeleton when `WorkspaceRegistry.create(template=<name>)`; `workspace.py` ships `WorkspaceLifecycleState` `IntEnum` (7 values 0..6: INITIATED/CONSULTING/PLAN_REVIEW/APPROVED/EXECUTING/COMPLETED/ARCHIVED) + `_ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS` adjacency map (PLAN_REVIEW can revert to CONSULTING for revision; ARCHIVED is terminal) + frozen `ConsultationWorkspaceSummary` dataclass (id/title/state/owner/participant_count/created_at/updated_at + `to_dict()`) + `ConsultationWorkspace` instance class with public id/root_path/manifest/lifecycle_state properties, six convenience writers each appending a journal entry (`add_input` routes through `InputProcessor.process` — bytes-in / bytes-out contract, decoded as utf-8 with `errors="replace"` for the raw write; `add_advisory(agent_id, content, *, summary="")` writes `{agent_id}_{ts_ms_13digit}.md` under `advisory/`; `add_plan_iteration(content)` scans `plan/` for `plan_v*.md` files, parses trailing integer, writes `plan_v{N+1}.md` — atomic filename-based versioning, no metadata file; `add_artifact`/`add_output(filename, content)` write under their respective dirs; `add_work_item(spec)` serializes spec dict via `yaml.safe_dump` to `wi_{spec.id_or_uuid8}.yaml`), `transition_to(state, *, agent_id="captain") -> bool` validates predecessor against `_ALLOWED_TRANSITIONS` (invalid → returns False + WARNING log, never raises; manifest unchanged on rejection), `append_journal(message, *, agent_id)` writes `- {ts:.3f} [{agent_id}] {message}\n` line via `RecordsStore.append_workspace_file` (best-effort log-and-degrade), `list_paths()` snapshots files under each subdirectory; private helpers `_next_plan_version()` / `_persist_manifest()` / module-private `_safe_filename_segment(s)` strips traversal characters from caller-supplied filenames (raises ValueError on empty post-strip); `WorkspaceRegistry(records_store, *, root_path="consultations", clock=time.time, input_processor=None)` ctor (raises ValueError if records_store is None), public async `create(*, title, owner_agent_id, participants, template=None) -> ConsultationWorkspace` (workspace_id = uuid4().hex[:12]; materializes 6 subdirectories by writing `.gitkeep` into each; persists initial manifest at schema_version=1 with INITIATED state; writes empty `delivery.yaml` placeholder for AD-594d; appends "workspace created" journal entry; applies template if recognized in TEMPLATES), `get(workspace_id)` with manifest-load cache, `list_active() -> list[ConsultationWorkspaceSummary]` filters out ARCHIVED; private `_load_manifest()` parses YAML with malformed-input log-and-degrade. Module-level `_WORKSPACE_SUBDIRS = ("inputs", "advisory", "plan", "artifacts", "outputs", "workitems")` + `_MANIFEST_SCHEMA_VERSION = 1`. Public package `__init__.py` re-exports 17 names. (3) **Pydantic config + finalize wirer** — new `ConsultationWorkspaceConfig` BaseModel (`enabled: bool = True`, `root_path: str = "consultations"`, `input_processor: str = "passthrough"`) declared adjacent to `ClinicalTelemetryConfig` at `config.py:1876`; wired on `SystemConfig` adjacent to `clinical_telemetry: ClinicalTelemetryConfig` at `:2226` via `consultation_workspaces: ConsultationWorkspaceConfig = Field(default_factory=ConsultationWorkspaceConfig)`. Default-True deviation from Wave-10 transitional-flag convention is INTENTIONAL — registry is read-only on boot (constructs an empty in-memory cache; only side-effect is `RecordsStore` ensuring `consultations/` directory exists at first init via `_SUBDIRS` mkdir); same precedent as `KnowledgeEdgesConfig` / `EdgeBackfillConfig`. New sync `_wire_consultation_workspaces(*, runtime, config) -> bool` wirer at `startup/finalize.py` mirrors `_wire_clinical_telemetry` shape; reads `runtime.records_store` (AD-434 adopted at `runtime.py:1324` cognitive phase, well before finalize); skips with INFO log if records_store unavailable; constructs `runtime.consultation_workspaces = WorkspaceRegistry(records_store, root_path=cfg.root_path, input_processor=build_input_processor(cfg.input_processor))` collision-free public attribute (verified). Invoked from `finalize_startup` immediately after `_wire_clinical_telemetry` and before `_wire_workspace_ontology` at `:815`. **What this AD does NOT change** (out of scope by design — separate GH issues, not deferrals): no AD-594b consultation primitive (`consult(question, context)` on CognitiveAgent — #161); no AD-594c parallel execution dispatch (`WorkItemStore` registration, dependency/conflict detection, multi-executor assignment — #162); no AD-594d delivery pipeline (`DeliveryAdapter`, Captain approval gate, format transformation, revision cycle — #163; v1 ships EMPTY `delivery.yaml` placeholder file only); no HXI message rendering integration (parser + renderer ship as pure helpers; `MessageStore.create_post()` wrapper is consumer-side task); no PDF/image/audio input processors (Protocol seam only; v1 = passthrough); no real `WorkItemStore` integration (`add_work_item` writes YAML spec to `workitems/`; does NOT register with `runtime.work_item_store`); no new EventType (workspace activity journaled to per-workspace `journal.md`; cross-mesh observability deferred AD-594b/c); no HXI surface (Captain-facing visualization deferred); no REST API endpoints (`/api/consultations` URL is future hook in `render_workspace_refs_md` default; no router shipped); no federation export (workspaces local to single ProbOS instance); no `ward_room/messages.py` or `ward_room/service.py` modification. 16 focused tests pass at `tests/test_ad594a_consultation_workspace.py` (over the 14 floor by 2): `WorkspaceLifecycleState` enum 7 values + integer ordering; `parse_workspace_refs` extracts 3 refs in order from text + empty + no-refs returns []; `parse_workspace_refs` case-insensitive duplicates preserved; `render_workspace_refs_md` substitutes single ref + empty passthrough + custom base_url; `WorkspaceRegistry.create` produces 6 subdirs + manifest.yaml + journal.md + delivery.yaml on disk + `list_paths()` returns 6 keys; manifest.yaml schema (schema_version=1, id, title, owner, participants list, lifecycle_state="INITIATED", created_at/updated_at floats, template=""); full happy-path lifecycle INITIATED → CONSULTING → PLAN_REVIEW → APPROVED → EXECUTING → COMPLETED → ARCHIVED all True with 6 lifecycle journal entries; lifecycle rejects EXECUTING → INITIATED (False, manifest unchanged, WARNING logged); PLAN_REVIEW → CONSULTING revision allowed; `add_input` routes through custom `_UpperProcessor` (filename rewritten to `report.txt.proc`, content uppercased); `add_advisory` writes `{agent_id}_{ts}.md` with advisory-report skeleton including summary + body; three `add_plan_iteration` calls produce `plan_v1.md`/`plan_v2.md`/`plan_v3.md`; `add_artifact` + `add_output` write to their respective dirs; `add_work_item` serializes spec YAML round-trips via `yaml.safe_load` with `wi_{id}.yaml` filename + uuid8 fallback when no id; journal appended on advisory + transition + add_input (3 lines delta); `list_active` filters ARCHIVED workspace + returns `ConsultationWorkspaceSummary` with correct state/participant_count for non-archived. Pre-commit deletion sanity: max 0 deletions any single tracked file (additive-only changes — three ===REPLACE=== blocks expand existing tuples/sections; new package + new test file). Full gate: 11094 passed, 15 skipped (delta +16 vs Wave 43 baseline 11078 — exact target hit). No hard-stops triggered. Closes GH issue #160.

AD-695 v1 CLOSED. Ship Health Oracle Tier 7 + Threshold Bridge Alerts (GH issue #389, Wave 43). Three converging components landed in one OSS v1: (1) **Oracle Tier 7 "health"** — new `OracleService._query_health(query_text, *, k)` reads runtime telemetry (pool stats via `spawner.pools[name].current_size/target_size`, attention queue depth via `attention.queue_size`, degradation tier via `degradation_manager.status().stress_level.value`, optional vitals via `observability_bridge.take_snapshot()`) and returns one `OracleResult` per metric scored by simple keyword overlap against the query, with `provenance="[health: <metric>]"`. `"health"` appended as the 7th element of the default `active_tiers` list (after `episodic`/`records`/`operational`/`archive`/`semantic`/`graph`); Tier 7 dispatch slot inserted between Tier 6 (graph) and `_expand_via_graph` so health results compete in expansion ranking. New ctor kwarg `health_provider: Any = None` plus public idempotent `attach_health_provider(provider)` setter mirroring `attach_semantic_layer`/`attach_knowledge_graph` shape. Health provider is duck-typed (anything exposing `spawner.pools` + `attention` + `degradation_manager` + optional `observability_bridge`); the runtime itself satisfies the protocol natively, tests inject `SimpleNamespace`. (2) **`ThresholdAlertService`** — new `src/probos/cognitive/threshold_alerts.py` with frozen `ThresholdAlert` dataclass (threshold_id/severity/metric/value/threshold/title/detail/fired_at + optional related_pool) and `ThresholdAlertService(runtime, *, pool_saturation_floor=0.9, degradation_min_severity="degraded", attention_queue_depth=20, dedup_window_seconds=300.0)`. Single public `async check_and_alert() -> list[ThresholdAlert]` runs three independently log-and-degrade checks (pool_saturation per pool, degradation via `StressLevel` ordering `{normal:0, elevated:1, degraded:2, critical:3}` with `severity="alert"` only at CRITICAL else `"advisory"`, attention queue depth) and posts each fired alert via `runtime.ward_room_router.deliver_bridge_alert(BridgeAlert(...))` with id=uuid4, alert_type=threshold_id, dedup_key=threshold_id, severity coerced to `AlertSeverity` enum. Per-(threshold_id) dedup ring with configurable sliding window, prunes entries older than 2x window. Service NEVER raises into caller — every check + every delivery is best-effort. (3) **Bridge loop becomes a threshold loop** — `ObservabilityBridge._publish_once` (BF-258 already nuked the `create_post()` spam) now invokes `await runtime.threshold_alerts.check_and_alert()` when wired, then preserves the existing `OBSERVABILITY_SNAPSHOT_PUBLISHED` event emit; `take_snapshot()` retained unchanged for both Tier 7 vitals augmentation and event-log subscribers. The bridge is no longer a heart with no pulse — it is the cadence trigger for threshold checks. New `ThresholdAlertConfig` Pydantic model (`enabled: bool = False`, `pool_saturation_floor: float = 0.9` validated [0,1], `degradation_min_severity: str = "degraded"`, `attention_queue_depth: int = 20` validated >=0, `dedup_window_seconds: float = 300.0` validated >=1) wired adjacent to `ObservabilityBridgeConfig` on `SystemConfig`. **Default-False is REQUIRED, NOT a deviation** — unlike AD-687/AD-689/AD-692 (default-True with pass-through-when-disabled cost), ThresholdAlerts actually post things on enable; operator opt-in. New finalize wirer block immediately after the AD-641a ObservabilityBridge block: constructs `runtime.threshold_alerts` when config.threshold_alerts.enabled, else None; ALSO unconditionally stitches `oracle.attach_health_provider(runtime)` for the Oracle Tier 7 late-bind (Tier 7 returns `[]` until this stitch runs). 13 focused tests pass at `tests/test_ad695_ship_health_oracle.py` (over the 12 floor by 1): default tier list contains `"health"` 7th; `_query_health` returns `[]` when no provider; pool happy path with `SimpleNamespace` provider (1 result, metric=pool, current=2/target=4, provenance=`[health: pool]`); attention queue metric (queue_depth=7); degradation metric (stress_level=degraded, shed_count=2); query keyword filter (`pool medical` returns pool-tier first); k-truncation (5 pools + attention + degradation, k=2 → exactly 2); `ThresholdAlertConfig` defaults; pool saturation breach fires (9/10, advisory, related_pool=medical, BridgeAlert dedup_key=`pool_saturation:medical`); degradation escalation fires at CRITICAL (severity=alert, AlertSeverity.ALERT); attention queue depth fires at 25; dedup prevents repost within window then fires again after window advances (3 calls → 1 fire / 0 fires / 1 fire; `deliver_bridge_alert` awaited 2 times); no-breach returns empty (5/10 pool + queue=0 + normal stress → no fires, `deliver_bridge_alert` not awaited). **What this AD does NOT change** (out of scope by design): no HXI/dashboard integration of Tier 7 (deferred AD-695b if data warrants); no historical retention of alerts beyond `bridge_alert` event row that `deliver_bridge_alert` already writes (existing AD-410 untouched); no per-agent health querying (Oracle context per-query only); no active shedding hooks (still AD-459b/AD-396); no AD-466 infrastructure restructuring (package layout untouched); no full AD-466c scope (audit log, retention, per-pool thresholds deferred); existing `BridgeAlertService` untouched (ThresholdAlertService constructs `BridgeAlert` directly, does not call `BridgeAlertService.check_*`); no new EventType. **Critical architect call (DLog #1)**: BF-258 ALREADY removed the spam `create_post()` call (BF-258 comment block at `bridge.py:121-124` mentions "Crew queries system health via Oracle (AD-695)" as the planned replacement); the "spam loop disable" task from the user spec was a NO-OP at HEAD. v1 replaces the dormant body with a guarded `await runtime.threshold_alerts.check_and_alert()` call — NOT a re-disable. **DLog #2**: `runtime` IS the duck-typed health_provider; finalize wirer calls `oracle.attach_health_provider(runtime)` after threshold_alerts wirer; OracleService at HEAD never reaches `runtime` directly (uses injected providers per AD-686/AD-688) — AD-695 mirrors that pattern. **DLog #6**: Pool surface reduced to current_size/target_size only — `Pool.info()` at HEAD does NOT expose cooldown counts (verified by grep); cooldown surfacing deferred (would require Pool.info() schema change). **DLog #7**: Tier 7 dispatch position critical — must run BEFORE `_expand_via_graph` (Tier 7 results compete in expansion ranking) and AFTER all 6 existing tiers; SEARCH anchor wraps the existing `_expand_via_graph` block. Phantom-API pre-check: 1 candidate (`SimpleNamespace` = stdlib FP test fixture), 2 standard intro-not-in-index skips (`runtime.threshold_alerts.check_and_alert`, `runtime.oracle.attach_health_provider`). 0 NEW phantoms. Same FP class as Waves 27–42. Full gate: 11078 passed, 15 skipped (delta +13 vs Wave 42 baseline 11065 — exact target hit). Pre-commit deletion sanity: max ~5 deletions any single file (line-level config.py + roadmap status flip + bridge.py comment-block replace). No hard-stops. Closes GH issue #389.

AD-692 v1 CLOSED. Classification Enforcement on Knowledge Graph Edges (GH issue #386, Wave 42). Phase B (Intelligence) of the Unified Knowledge Graph + Oracle Unification stack — closes the **read/write authorization seam** between AD-687 `KnowledgeEdgeStore` and consumers (AD-688 Oracle Tier 6) by adding a decorator-pattern wrapper (`ClassificationGatedKnowledgeEdgeStore`) around `SQLiteKnowledgeEdgeStore` that enforces the existing `KnowledgeEdge.classification` field at the storage boundary. OSS extension point — commercial overlays (audit, RBAC, multi-tenant) layer on top of the stable `KnowledgeEdgeStorage` Protocol seam without modifying the underlying store. New `src/probos/knowledge/edge_classification.py` ships: `ClassificationLevel` typed `IntEnum` with integer values matching `records_store._CLASSIFICATION_LEVELS` byte-for-byte (`PRIVATE=0`/`DEPARTMENT=1`/`SHIP=2`/`FLEET=3`) plus `from_label(label) -> ClassificationLevel` coercion that defaults to PRIVATE on `None`/unknown (most restrictive defensive); pure `edge_visible_to(edge, *, requester_tier, requester_agent_id) -> bool` visibility helper (PRIVATE = ownership gate via `edge.source_agent == requester_agent_id`; DEPARTMENT/SHIP/FLEET = tier-based via `_TIER_MAX_VISIBLE` mapping {basic: PRIVATE, enhanced: DEPARTMENT, full: SHIP, oracle: FLEET}); `KnowledgeEdgeClassificationGate` decision service with three async methods (`filter_edges(edges, *, requester_agent_id) -> list[KnowledgeEdge]` for read filtering, `authorize_write(edge, *, writer_agent_id) -> bool` for write gating where writers may always create PRIVATE edges they own and DEPARTMENT/SHIP/FLEET writes require read access at that tier — a writer cannot label content above their clearance) plus pure synchronous `filter_for_export(edges, *, target_classification=ClassificationLevel.SHIP) -> list[KnowledgeEdge]` extension hook (default excludes FLEET-tagged edges from cross-mesh export; not consumed in v1 since federation does not export edges at HEAD); `ClassificationGatedKnowledgeEdgeStore` decorator wrapper around any `KnowledgeEdgeStorage` implementation that adds optional `requester_agent_id` kwarg to `find_edges`/`traverse`/`get_edge` (when `None` — the default and the case for all Wave 37/38/39/40/41 tests — the wrapper is a transparent pass-through; when set, results are filtered through the gate); `add_edge` authorization-gates non-system writes (writes with `edge.source_agent` set) and returns the edge id idempotently when blocked (caller cannot distinguish from a stored edge — the in-store `find_edges(limit=100)` call confirms non-persistence). `traverse` uses **per-path drop semantics** — a path is dropped ENTIRELY if ANY hop edge fails visibility (conservative; prevents partial-path inference leakage). **Architect call (DLog #1) — AD-679 SHIPPED but ORTHOGONAL**: verified at `mesh/disclosure.py:15`. AD-679 is 5-tier `DisclosureLevel` IntEnum (PUBLIC..CLASSIFIED) for IntentMessage recipient routing. AD-692 is 4-tier `ClassificationLevel` IntEnum (PRIVATE..FLEET) for KnowledgeEdge read/write gating. **Different taxonomies by design** — recipient clearance vs. data-scope-of-audience. Bridging intentionally NOT in v1; `filter_for_export` ships as documented seam for future commercial overlay or AD-692c bridging work. **Architect call (DLog #2)**: `ClassificationLevel.from_label(None|unknown)` defaults to PRIVATE (most restrictive defensive) so pre-existing edges from Wave 37/38/39/40/41 that omitted classification stay restricted unless an explicit owner-equality match passes. **Architect call (DLog #3) — per-department matching DEFERRED to AD-692b**: `KnowledgeEdge` has no department field at HEAD (verified 13-field shape `edges.py:73-96`). v1 DEPARTMENT gate is tier-based only (any ENHANCED+ requester sees all department-classified edges). Documented as out-of-scope. **Architect call (DLog #4) — resolver injection via setter** (Open/Closed + layer discipline): `knowledge/edge_classification.py` does NOT import `earned_agency` or `ontology` directly. Wirer in `startup/finalize.py` builds a closure over `runtime.ontology` + `runtime.clearance_grant_store` + `runtime.registry` and injects via `gate.set_clearance_resolver(resolver)`. Mirrors AD-660/AD-635 pattern. **Architect call (DLog #5) — wirer phase ordering**: must run in finalize. `runtime.knowledge_edges` adopted at `runtime.py:1618`, `runtime.ontology` at `:1651`. Wirer ALSO re-stitches Oracle Tier 6 via `oracle.attach_knowledge_graph(wrapper)` so Tier 6 sees the wrapper, not the bare AD-687 store. New `KnowledgeEdgeClassificationConfig` Pydantic model (`enabled=True`, `default_classification="private"` validated via `field_validator` against {private/department/ship/fleet}) wired adjacent to `knowledge_edges`/`edge_backfill` on `SystemConfig`. Default-True deviation from Wave-10 transitional-flag convention is intentional + same precedent as `KnowledgeEdgesConfig`/`EdgeBackfillConfig` — wrapper is transparent pass-through when no `requester_agent_id` supplied; cost = wrapper construction at boot. New `_wire_edge_classification(*, runtime, config) -> bool` sync wirer at `startup/finalize.py` mirrors `_wire_nl_graph_query` shape; constructs gate, builds tier resolver closure (registry lookup → agent_type/rank → `resolve_billet_clearance(agent_type, ontology)` + `resolve_active_grants(agent_id, clearance_grant_store)` → `effective_recall_tier(rank, billet, grants).value` — RecallTier is `str, Enum` so `.value` is the lowercase string form; tier-2 log-and-degrade returns "basic" on any resolver failure), wraps `runtime.knowledge_edges`, sets `runtime.edge_classification_gate` public attribute (collision-free — verified against `_classification_gate` AD-530 in `security/classification.py`), re-attaches the wrapper to Oracle Tier 6 via existing `oracle.attach_knowledge_graph(wrapper)`. Invoked from `finalize_startup` immediately after `_wire_nl_graph_query` and before `_wire_clinical_telemetry`. Oracle Tier 6 plumbed through: `OracleService.query()`'s existing `agent_id` kwarg now threads to `_query_graph(query_text, *, k, requester_agent_id="")` (default empty string preserves Wave 38 backward-compat — no filtering when empty); `_query_graph` calls go through two new private helpers `_graph_find_edges`/`_graph_traverse` that pass `requester_agent_id` only when non-empty AND fall back via `TypeError` catch when the underlying graph instance does NOT accept the kwarg (production wraps see the AD-692 wrapper which accepts the kwarg; MagicMock-based Wave 38 tests fall back gracefully — same backward-compat invariant as the wrapper itself). Knowledge package `__init__.py` exports the 4 new names (`ClassificationGatedKnowledgeEdgeStore`, `ClassificationLevel`, `KnowledgeEdgeClassificationGate`, `edge_visible_to`) alongside existing AD-687/AD-689/AD-690 names. **What this AD does NOT change** (out of scope by design): no new EventType (audit emission deferred AD-692b); no persistence of denied-access audit log (in-memory ring buffer also deferred AD-692b — would couple gate to event bus); no federation export of edges (`filter_for_export` ships as pure helper / extension hook only — federation today does not export `KnowledgeEdge` objects; future work AD); no bridging to AD-679 `DisclosureLevel` (intentionally orthogonal taxonomies; AD-692c if commercial overlay needs unified policy); no per-department classification matching (`KnowledgeEdge` has no `dept` field; v1's DEPARTMENT gate is tier-based only); no encryption-at-rest for edge fields; no fine-grained per-edge ACLs beyond level-based gating; no agent / pool / intent (this is a guard layer; existing callers consume it transparently); no HXI surface (Captain-facing visibility deferred AD-692d). 21 focused tests pass at `tests/test_ad692_classification_enforcement.py` (over the 12 floor by 9 — the parametrized matrix in test #2 fans out to 8 cases across tier × classification × ownership): `ClassificationLevel` enum ordering + `from_label` round-trip + None/empty/unknown coercion to PRIVATE; 8-case matrix of `edge_visible_to` (basic+private+owner=requester True; basic+private+other False; basic+department False; enhanced+department True; enhanced+ship False; full+ship True; full+fleet False; oracle+fleet True); gate filter_edges with ORACLE clearance returns mixed 4-classification list; gate filter_edges drops fleet for FULL clearance (3/4 returned, no fleet); gate authorize_write blocks ENHANCED writer attempting FLEET edge; gate authorize_write permits any-tier writer adding own PRIVATE edge; gate filter_for_export default `target=SHIP` excludes fleet (3/4) plus explicit `target=FLEET` returns all 4; wrapper find_edges with ENHANCED requester filters real `SQLiteKnowledgeEdgeStore`-backed fixture (private-owned + department visible, ship blocked); wrapper find_edges with no requester is pass-through (3/3 — backward compat); wrapper traverse drops paths containing FLEET hop entirely (per-path drop semantics) for FULL-clearance requester; wrapper edge with `classification=None` treated as PRIVATE — not returned to non-owner, returned to owner; wrapper add_edge blocks unauthorized SHIP write by basic-tier writer (returned id idempotent surface, but `inner.find_edges(limit=100)` confirms non-persistence); Oracle Tier 6 `_query_graph(query_text="alice", k=10, requester_agent_id="alice")` against ENHANCED-clearance gate-wrapped store returns FEWER results than unfiltered baseline (4 → 3, fleet edge filtered); backward-compat smoke wraps `SQLiteKnowledgeEdgeStore` populated with 5 edges of varying classifications and verifies pre-wrap and post-wrap counts of `find_edges()` and `traverse()` (no `requester_agent_id`) match exactly — wrapper is a no-op pass-through for legacy callers. Phantom-API pre-check at draft time: 3 candidates ALL FPs (`runtime.edge_classification_gate` — introduced by prompt; `add_edge(classification=)` + `add_edge(source_agent=)` — script matching nested `KnowledgeEdge` constructor kwargs in test snippets, both verified fields on `KnowledgeEdge` dataclass). 0 NEW phantoms. Same FP class as Waves 27–41.
